- 2026-10-10 tick: MULTI-ARG KERNELS WORK. add3 128/128 correct (fence 0.17 ms). Root cause of the hang: IGC's prolog loads cross-thread bytes 32.. with load.ugm.d32x8t.a32 bti[255] at (r0.0 & ~63) + CROSS_THREAD_OFFSET_OFF_R0, i.e. A32 relative to General State Base (0), and r0.0 = IndirectDataStartAddress; old batch had start 0 + IOH base 0x4_0000_0000 -> load from VA 0 -> fault/stall. Fix: IOH base 0, IndirectDataStartAddress = 64-aligned VA below 4 GB (0x8000_0000), length 32; reloc stays 0 (indirect layout = cross-thread remainder, per-thread data after at +0x20 only when SW local IDs). gen_batch.c argv[7] = IND_START (local copy ~/intek-arc-b70/usb/pack/gen_batch.c); ISA from IGC_ShaderDumpEnable in <other-host> ~/b70-ocloc/igcdump.
- 2026-10-10 tick: GENERAL KERNEL LOADER + PYTHON BATCH PACKING. ~/intek-arc-b70/usb/genxml.py packs GFX20 commands straight from Mesa's xe2.xml (+gen125/120/110 imports); byte-identical to the C/Mesa packer for add3 (352 B). zebin.py: ELF + .ze_info parser, payload layout (global_id_offset, local sizes, num groups, global size, work dim, stateless pointers, by-value), inline/indirect split, batch builder. b70.Runtime: VM VA allocators (low <4 GB for indirect data), load(), launch(). test_add3.py on the B70: fill2 4096/4096, add3 4096/4096 at local 32/64/128 (1/2/4 threads per group), 4 launches in one context, fence 0.17 ms, 2.2 ms per launch over USB. <other-host> now only needed for ocloc compile. zebin_text() removed (callers -> zebin.Kernel).
- 2026-10-10 link experiments (~/intek-arc-b70/usb/link_exp.py F/A/D, link_trace.py, lane_slices.py; scout report agent LinkGen1x1): (F) ASM link block B403 01 B431 0c B432 07 B455 19 B481 03 E764 1c E765 07 C659 01 - no hidden Gen1 latch visible. (A) ASM2824-quirk order (TLS 2.5 + retrain, wait DLLLA, then TLS Gen2/Gen3 + retrain): Gen1 x1. (D) E764 0x1c->0x19 (stock trained) + TLS Gen2/3 retrain: Gen1 x1 (restored 0x1c). Trace: LBMS clears, retrain sets it again (retrain runs), but polling B450 every ~0.2 ms for 1 s after a Gen2-directed retrain never leaves L0 (0x78): Recovery.Speed is never entered, i.e. the rate change is not even attempted on the wire (a failed attempt dwells ms in Recovery and logs errors; AER/lane errors stay 0). Lane slices: lane 1 is powered and active (analog regs 0x53/0x54/0x59-0x5b/0xba-0xbc move, unlike disabled lanes 2-3) but lacks status bits lane 0 has (0x51 0x11 vs 0x01, 0x7d 0x0c vs 0x04, 0xe7 0x03 vs 0x01) -> lane 1 never locks with the card. Not an idle downshift: LABS=0, no driver/pcode speed control exists on BMG (xe_pcode_api.h only has the read-only Gen5->Gen4 auto-downgrade). Gen3 cap = dock firmware B403=1 by design (Gen4 EQ unreliable on the authors' board); B403=0 advertises Gen4, moot until upshift works. Leading cause: card upstream port (e2ff) not advertising >2.5 GT/s / lane 1 not training with this partner - matches Intel 'Arc stuck at PCIe 1.0 x1' reports fixed by an IFWI update (Windows Arc Pro driver or Linux igsc, kernel >= 6.17). Needs the user: B70 in a normal PCIe slot once (read e2ff LnkSta there; update IFWI), then back on the dock.

- 2026-10-11T16:15Z (HandshakeAndConvert) H2D FIX + REAL ROOT CAUSE. (1) tgcu.py AsyncCopy.h2d rewritten to tinygrad's upload order: CE job queued FIRST behind a channel semaphore acquire (NVC56F SEM_EXECUTE acquire, 64-bit payload) on a sentinel = the last 8 bytes of the bulk payload at wire-8 (wire = payload + 8 rounded to 512; a unique 0x5A17C0DE<<32|seq each push, nothing cleared), then 0xF2 arm, then bulk OUT; payload releases the CE, no BAR write after the payload. Order matters: arm BEFORE submit drops the transfer (gate never opens, async copy timeout); submit -> arm -> bulk works. Window per round = whole SRAM (TRANSFER_SIZE-512). Orig ~/strata-patches/orig-1011-h2dsem/tgcu.py, new ~/strata-patches/tgcu.py.h2dgate (= ~/shim/tgcu.py). The microbench NEVER reproduced the 13-15 ms push (old order: 1.03 ms busy), the new order did not change it in the engine either (R4: 888/460/473 ms/window vs R1 791/510/463). (2) ROOT CAUSE of the 13-15 ms h2d _submit BAR writes (and of the 15.5 ms/layer handshake): the CPU expert pool's workers SPIN (_mm_pause, STRATA_POOL_SPIN_US default = 20 ms before sleeping; pool.cpp) on all 10 cores right after each layer's compute, so the host thread doing USB (libusb control/bulk transfers, BAR writes) is starved of CPU; d2h came before the pool started, h2d after. (1) USB LINK IS HALF DUPLEX through the ASM2464 bridge (mini, idle card, experiments/duplex/*.py, runs/pfpipe/): stock one-direction 10 MB: h2d 233 MB/s, d2h 157 MB/s. Per-step costs of one round (duplex_prof.py, medians): up 392 KB = 1.69 ms = CE-job submit 0.65 (4 BAR pokes over USB) + arm 0.08 + bulk OUT 0.44 (0.9 GB/s) + CE wait 0.50; down 256 KB = 1.56 ms = arm 0.06 + submit 0.68 + CE->SRAM 0.34 + bulk IN 0.47 (wire = 80 KB prefix + payload: the read always starts at SRAM slot 0; a read armed at start slot 5 times out). Two outstanding uploads (alternate SRAM regions) only +0-16% (62.8/118.5/164 vs 62/102/~140 MB/s at 64/128/190 KB rounds): the fixed per-round pokes dominate. DUPLEX: a second copy channel on COPY1 works (down 105 MB/s at 128 KB). Arming an upload AND a download back to back (either order): the download is exact, the upload's sentinel never opens (dropped) in 3/3 runs. Staggered (download armed 200 us after the upload's bulk OUT started): both exact, combined 104.0 MB/s vs 104.2 MB/s sequential (wall 0.161 s for 2 x 8 MB in both): zero overlap; other order drops the upload / corrupts the first download round. => full duplex does NOT hold; the cross-chunk wavefront (down+up concurrently) is not buildable on this bridge. (2) PIPELINED TRANSFERS (tgcu.py AsyncCopy._h2d_pipe/_d2h_pipe, tgcu_api.py in-place buffers, env TGCU_PIPE_MIN default 1 MiB, 0 = stock): ONE pushbuffer + one doorbell holds the CE jobs of the whole message; uploads alternate two 12-slot SRAM regions (sentinel-gated jobs, poll for the job two rounds back), downloads queue one hold-gated job per 400 KB round (host: arm, one poke, bulk IN). 10 MB: h2d 228 -> 353 MB/s, d2h 154 -> 328 MB/s, byte-exact over 24 random transfers + small-op regression; sweep up regions 6/9/12 slots 231/302/353, down rounds 256/320/400 KB 278/310/328. ROOT CAUSES (two, both reproduced): (1) the 'GPU timeout waiting for semaphore' was a tgcu SELF-DEADLOCK in Context._transient: `_submit(self._pushbuf(..))` runs _pushbuf first (sem_value += 1 for the pushbuffer being built) and then `_transient(len(pb))`, which at the end of the 16 MiB transient ring did `_wait(self.sem_value)` = wait for the release of a pushbuffer that cannot be queued until _transient returns -> 10 s timeout with compute sem = want-1, CE idle, no MMU/SM fault reported, last launch arbitrary (tgcu_memcpy/memset). It fires only when the PUSHBUFFER (not the QMD batch) crosses the ring end, i.e. rarely and workload-phase dependent (long prompts = many wraps: 33K prompt ~5, decode after it), in any config - G=4 only hit it first (2 of 3 needle runs: once in prefill at moe_combine_peer16's h2d->flush, once in decode graph_launch at token 53; engine logs with TGCU_HANG_DUMP: ~/strata-tgcu-shim/runs/g4place/g4_s1_hang_excerpt.txt). Deterministic repro without the engine: test_wrap.py old (old logic, ring sized so the pushbuffer allocation crosses the end) = `GPU timeout waiting for semaphore 42` after 10.0 s. FIX (~/strata-patches/g4fix/tgcu.diff, deployed ~/shim/tgcu.py, backup orig-1012-g4/tgcu.py): the transient ring is two segments used in turn, entering a segment waits only for seg_last[segment] (the semaphore value of the last submission that wrote it, recorded after the doorbell) - never for the submission being built nor for a graph spinning on a host handshake; test_wrap.py new: 20,000 flushes (~500 wraps of the small ring) ok. Also new: TGCU_HANG_DUMP=1 / TGCU_WAIT_S=N diagnostics (thread stacks, semaphores, CE/compute state, handshake words, fault readout, native `sample` per dump; tgcu_api_hang_dump.diff). 'Earlier ring firing' does not exist: layer l's router input is layer l's attention output, so the ring cannot fire before the GPU finishes attention(l); the 17-29 ms GPU-reach wait IS the GPU's attention/GDN/VRAM-expert time. A warm-ahead thread (PleTable::warm_rows, STRATA_PF_PLE_WARM_AHEAD default 12, only chunks after the first lockstep group) removes it for 18K/33K (44.7 -> 42.3 s, 80.3 -> 77.0 s) but cannot for the first group (SSD IOPS ~117K/s shared with the expert reads: 8K stays 20.1 -> 19.3 s). (2) STEADY STATE IS NOT SSD- OR GPU-BOUND, IT IS THE USB HOST THREAD: TGCU_PROF on an 8K prompt: host inside tgcu API ~94% of the period; per chunk-layer (192 total) x_fetch (lazy_fetch) 31.5 ms + y push (h2d) ~36 ms + ~21 ms of other API (flush 5.6, synchronize 4.4, launch+order 6, small d2h 2.3, malloc/free of the lazy shadow 2.3, lazy_submit 1.2) = the 88 ms period; link bytes 20.5 MB per chunk-layer at 340 MB/s. SSD: 491 MB/layer at 2.1 GB/s = 233 ms/layer, hidden for G=4 (352 ms), balanced at G=3 (282), binding at G=2 (2.5K: 226 ms x 2 chunks = the 11.3 s pass = 226 tok/s; threads 16 vs 8 no change: 13839 vs 13847 ms). A 2.5K prompt cannot reach 600: one pass over the cold experts is ~11 s at 2.1 GB/s (iostat 1.9-2.05 GB/s), 2.8-3.0 GB/s peak in nvme_bench. 600 needs period <= ~68 ms at G>=4 (and >= 4 chunks per pass); max with zero overhead at today's link speed = 632.
  NEGATIVE / NOT-A-LEVER: STRATA_PF_MAC_THREADS=16 (no gain); TGCU_LAZY_MIN_MB=16 (new libcudart: lane x copy not lazy: 8K 20256/17556 ms = same as baseline; reverted to the old dylib); polling cost in the upload pipeline: per round poll 0.16 ms + arm 0.075 + bulk 0.24 (192 KB) -> 380 MB/s; skipping polls would give 450-550 MB/s (up_prof.py) but is unsafe (CE job may still drain the region) - a helper-thread poller collapses throughput to 45 MB/s; XDATA scan: GPU PCIe writes to 0x828000+off land in registers 0xB2xx/0xB3xx (not a cheap-poll mailbox). Not run: SPLITF_MIN=3, L0-L2 pre-warm (the cold gap is PLE, L0 read is 0.14-0.18 s), batching/wait work beyond the profile above.
  ONLY ROUTE TO >=600 FOUND (not built): (a) 8-bit block-32 transport of the lane's x (GPU->host) and y (host->GPU) rows: halves the link bytes (x/y 5.6 MB vs 10.2 MB), ~-30 ms of the 88 ms period; needs 2 small CUDA kernels (quant on the 3090, dequant on the 3090; CPU NEON quant/dequant on the Mac), nvcc exists on <other-host>/2384 (cuda-13.0 aarch64; the mini cannot run its nvcc: ELF) and the mac build links fatbin + clang --cuda-host-only (host.ninja rule cuhost); numerics: ~0.5% per-element noise vs 8-10% from the 3 bpw weights, engine already uses int8 activations on the fused IQ path; mac_y_dev's unused second half (y16 mode) can hold the y q8 buffer, x q8 needs a new T*N*17/16 buffer counted in bytes_needed_impl. (b) THEN the Metal job (~74 ms GPU per chunk-layer incl. ~56 ms weight decode repeated for each chunk of a group; stats line now prints mgpu) becomes the cap at ~570 tok/s: needs a decode shared across chunks. Both are quality/engineering decisions for Main.

  T5 CEILING MODEL (runs/decode5/ceiling; fitted on 24 measured requests, served 7,257 + borrow 8,393 slots, rms residual 4% of the per-ring chain): W[ms/window] = Wfix + 48*(f0 + a*n + s*m), Wfix = 25.8 ms (GPU-reach wait 15-18 + draft 7.5-9.5 + commit/stage 1.8), f0 = 0.692 ms per ring (USB handshake + Metal job floor), a = 41.5 us per cold expert (Metal), s = 0.834 ms per mini-LRU miss (1.87 MB SSD read, ~2.2 GB/s), n = cold experts/ring, m = misses/ring; tok/s = 1000*tau/W. Served: n 12.4, m 1.45 -> W 142 ms, tau 2.7 -> 19.0 tok/s (measured 17-22). Mini at 100% hit (m=0, n unchanged) -> W 83.7 -> 32.3 tok/s (per probe 36.1 / 28.1 / 24.1); measured with identical repeated prompts: p2 21.2->27.0 tok/s, p1 stays 17-18 (its cold working set > 5,600 slots, misses stay ~2.2/ring). Card at 100% hit: W = Wfix 25.8 ms -> 77-128 tok/s (upper bound). 33 tok/s needs (avg tau 2.7, W<=81.8, per-ring budget 1.17 ms) 0.0415 n + 0.834 m <= 0.478: e.g. VRAM 12,000 slots + mini LRU 8,400 (n 7.9, m 0.17 -> 33.4 tok/s in-sample), or VRAM 15,000 + LRU 5,600 (34.8); the d33a ranking needs 12,000/8,400 for 37.9 in-sample. 12,000 slots = ~21 GiB of VRAM (now 14.6 GiB incl. borrow), impossible next to 200K KV on a 24 GB card. Per probe, 33 on the story/MoE prompts needs f0 <= 0.23/0.46 ms even at m=0: the per-ring handshake (33 ms/window) alone eats the budget. => 33 needs a ~3x cheaper per-ring handshake or an all-resident (bigger-VRAM) card; slots/hit tuning on this card tops out near 24-28 tok/s. Tables: runs/decode5/ceiling/ceil_out.txt.

ROOT CAUSE of the 'fast 3.3 / slow 2.05 GB/s' states of Decode11: the FAST state is UBC hits. ssd3 with a fixed random sequence: the first ~7,000 draws of seed 0 ran 2,850-2,880 MB/s for exactly 4.7 s then 2,230 (skip=7000: slow from t=0, skip=3500: fast for 2.3 s), no other seed was ever fast (seeds 1,2,5 x 3-16 repeats: 2,180-2,250); mincore: seed 0's first 7,000 blobs 41.5% resident in the file cache vs 17.9% for seed 5 and 2.4% after hog (whole file 18% = 8.2 GB resident after my own unaligned runs); after eviction seed 0 = 1,900-1,930 MB/s from t=0. F_NOCACHE does NOT bypass already-cached pages, and UNALIGNED F_NOCACHE preads populate the cache (resident 0.8% -> 18.6% after a grid with align=0 runs) while 4 KiB-aligned ones do not (2.4% stayed 2.4%): that is also why Decode11's aligned reads were 'faster' and why a repeated ssdbench2 blob sequence (srand 12345) looked fast for minutes and slow after the engine had run (the engine evicts the cache with 12 GB wired). Net per ring: unit -20..-120 us in rows-back vs +40..+270 us in reads, split -110 vs +500; the Metal job after the last landing is floor 135-210 + GPU 34-130 + completion detection 70 (fixed Metal latencies), not compute.

The critical path of a miss ring is read time (1.0 ms per blob at ~1.9 GB/s) + job B; of an in-place ring the Metal job A (commit-to-start + GPU + completion = 754 us from begin vs 584 us of host work).

  WHAT IT IS: the engine already had an elastic K/V (--kv-grow, generate.cpp kvg_*): the K/V pools and the expert cache are VMM ranges (cuMem*), the pools hold physical memory only for the cells a request reaches (init 16,384 cells, step 8,192), the cache gets the rest, and when a context grows the cache gives up slots just below the prompt path's loan (hotter experts move into colder slots first; the given-up experts become ordinary mini-lane misses) and their 2 MiB chunks are mapped into the pools; the next short request trims and refills the slots from the profile. It was OFF here for two reasons, both fixed: (1) the tgcu shim has no driver API (cudaGetDriverEntryPoint -> NotSupported, so vmm_available() = false): Decode14 adds the cuMem* emulation = tgcudart.cpp entry points (cuDeviceGetAttribute VMM, cuMemGetAllocationGranularity 2 MiB, AddressReserve/Free, Create/Release, Map/Unmap, SetAccess; reserved ranges registered in g_dev; TGCU_VMM=0 hides them) + tgcu.Context.vmm_* (VA from tinygrad's MemoryManager.alloc_vaddr, physical 2 MiB chunks from palloc(zero=False), PTEs written by a batched writer: tinygrad's map_range/unmap_range cost 1.6 ms per chunk over USB (12 ms single), the shim queues Map/Unmap and writes ONE bulk PTE store per page-table page at cuMemSetAccess / the next create-release-free: 600 chunks map in 17.5 ms; one GPU TLB flush per batch; unmap syncs the GPU first) + mem_info counts the VMM chunks (tools/vmm_test.py: kernel write/read through fresh pages, remap of a VA to another physical chunk, kept chunks intact, double map / bad unmap rejected, pool accounting back to 0: PASS); (2) generate.cpp gated --kv-grow off for the resident RAM mode (--resident-budget-gib sets resident_cpu_experts): STRATA_KV_GROW_RESIDENT=1 lifts that gate (patches/generate.cpp.d14kvg.diff; under the EXL3 lane a given-up expert is served by the mini like any non-resident one). Nothing else in the engine changed (binary = Decode12 objects + the new generate.cpp.o).
